## GHSA-pq96-jpmf-w254 · quasar

2026-10-07 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 10.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→XSS`

### Root cause

The Quasar Framework's Server-Side Rendering (SSR) mechanism for meta tags (like title, meta, link, script, noscript) did not properly escape user-controlled input before rendering it into the HTML head. This allowed attackers to inject arbitrary HTML and JavaScript.

### Impact

An attacker could inject malicious scripts into web pages, leading to session hijacking, defacement, redirection, or other client-side attacks against users viewing the affected pages.

### Key diff

```
--- a/ui/src/plugins/meta/Meta.js
+++ b/ui/src/plugins/meta/Meta.js
@@ -148,15 +190,17 @@ function apply({ add, remove }) {
 
 function getAttr(seed) {
   return att => {
+    if (isValidAttrName(att) === false) return
+
     const val = seed[att]
-    return att + (val !== true && val !== void 0 ? `="${val}"` : '')
+    return att + (val !== true && val !== void 0 ? `="${encodeHtml(val)}"` : '')
   }
 }
 
 function getHead(meta) {
   let output = ''
   if (meta.title) {
-    output += `<title>${meta.title}</title>`
+    output += `<title>${encodeHtml(meta.title)}</title>`
   }
   ;['meta', 'link', 'script'].forEach(type => {
     const metaType = meta[type]
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces several HTML and JSON encoding functions (`encodeHtml`, `encodeJson`, `protectRawText`) and a validation function for attribute names (`isValidAttrName`). These functions are applied to all user-controlled data rendered within meta tags, attributes, and script contents to ensure proper escaping and prevent injection.

## GHSA-r488-j9vj-wx3q · @payloadcms/plugin-form-builder

2026-10-07 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 10.0 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTHZ→RESOURCE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from overly permissive access control configurations in the Payload Form Builder plugin. Specifically, the 'read' access for 'FormSubmissions' and the 'emails' field within 'Forms' collections was set to allow any logged-in user to read, rather than restricting it to administrative users.

### Impact

An attacker, if authenticated as any user (not necessarily an admin), could read sensitive form submission data and email configurations, potentially leading to information disclosure or further attacks.

### Key diff

```
-      read: ({ req: { user } }) => !!user, // logged-in users,
+      read: ({ req }) => req.user?.collection === req.payload.config.admin.user,
```

### Fix summary

The patch tightens access control by changing the 'read' access logic. Instead of allowing any logged-in user, it now explicitly checks if the logged-in user belongs to the admin user collection configured in Payload CMS, thereby restricting access to administrative users only.

## GHSA-6xp5-7rcx-xfgx · github.com/sipcapture/homer-app

2026-10-07 · Go · **CRITICAL** 9.8 · Pattern: `CREDENTIAL_LEAK→HARDCODED`

### Root cause

The application used a hardcoded default administrator password 'sipcapture' when the internal authentication mechanism was enabled and no custom password was provided. This default password was also pre-filled in the setup wizard, making it easy for users to overlook changing it.

### Impact

An attacker could gain full administrative access to the Homer application by using the known hardcoded password, leading to complete compromise of the system and data.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/cli/wizard_cmd.go
+++ b/src/cli/wizard_cmd.go
@@ -417,8 +417,8 @@ func newWizardModel(outputPath string) wizardModel {
 	inputs[wfAdminUser].SetValue("admin")
 
 	inputs[wfAdminPass].Prompt = "Admin Password:  "
-	inputs[wfAdminPass].Placeholder = "sipcapture"
-	inputs[wfAdminPass].SetValue("sipcapture")
+	inputs[wfAdminPass].Placeholder = "(auto-generated if empty)"
+	inputs[wfAdminPass].SetValue("")
```

### Fix summary

The patch removes the hardcoded default password. Instead, if no password is provided during setup or for the bootstrap admin user, a strong random password is automatically generated. This generated password is then logged once for the user to retrieve, and users are prompted to change it after the first login.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 2 occurrences**
> Languages: Go
> → GHSA-w72w-9qmj-c9qm

🔗 [View commit](https://github.com/sipcapture/homer/commit/b2e942031ff8cd7435a244ebef306ee97d16b809)

## GHSA-f7hx-52q9-hcrf · payload

2026-10-07 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 9.8 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTHZ→RESOURCE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the orderable endpoint, which allows reordering of collection documents, did not properly enforce update access controls. While it checked for general update access, it did not apply the full access control logic, including `where` constraints, to each individual document being reordered. This allowed an attacker to reorder documents they did not have explicit update access to, as long as they had general update access to the collection.

### Impact

An attacker could reorder documents within a collection even if their access controls (specifically `where` conditions) should have prevented them from updating those specific documents. This could lead to unauthorized manipulation of data order, potentially affecting application logic or user experience.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/payload/src/config/orderable/index.ts
+++ b/packages/payload/src/config/orderable/index.ts
@@ -167,19 +169,6 @@ export const addOrderableEndpoint = (
       })
     }
 
-    // Prevent reordering if user doesn't have editing permissions
-    if (collection.access?.update) {
-      await executeAccess(
-        {
-          // Currently only one doc can be moved at a time. We should review this if we want to allow
-          // multiple docs to be moved at once in the future.
-          id: docsToMove[0],
-          data: {},
-          req,
-        },
-        collection.access.update,
-      )
-    }
     /**
      * If there is no target.key, we can assume the user enabled `orderable`
      * on a collection with existing documents, and that this is the first
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new `assertUpdateAccess` function that explicitly checks update access for each document involved in the reordering operation. This function now correctly applies `where` access results to ensure that only documents the user is authorized to update can be reordered. Additionally, `overrideAccess: false` is explicitly set in `payload.update` calls to ensure access controls are always enforced.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 127 occurrences**

## GHSA-rqcc-94gv-wjm9 · github.com/sipcapture/homer-app

2026-10-07 · Go · **CRITICAL** 9.8 · Pattern: `INSECURE_DEFAULT→CONFIG`

### Root cause

The Homer application allowed the 'coordinator.jwt.secret' configuration to be empty by default. When this secret was not explicitly configured, the application's JWT middleware would effectively disable authentication for protected API routes, allowing unauthenticated access.

### Impact

An attacker could completely bypass authentication and gain full access to all protected API endpoints, potentially leading to unauthorized data access, modification, or other critical actions.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/coordinator/coordinator.go
+++ b/src/coordinator/coordinator.go
@@ -98,9 +98,7 @@ func New(cfg *config.CoordinatorConfig) (*Coordinator, error) {
 
 	// Protected routes (JWT required) - v3
 	protected := c.echo.Group("/api/v3")
-	if c.config.JWT.Secret != "" {
-		protected.Use(authHandler.JWTMiddleware())
-	}
+	protected.Use(authHandler.JWTMiddleware())
 	{
 		// Search endpoints
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new service to ensure a JWT secret is always present. If 'coordinator.jwt.secret' is empty, a random secret is generated, persisted to a file, and used. Additionally, the JWT middleware now explicitly returns an internal server error if the secret is not configured, preventing unauthenticated access to protected routes.

## GHSA-g2v8-7jhw-pp8p · @backstage/plugin-scaffolder-backend

2026-10-07 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 9.6 · Pattern: `INFO_DISCLOSURE→ERROR_MESSAGE`

### Root cause

The API endpoints for listing and retrieving Scaffolder tasks were directly returning the full task object, which included sensitive information like 'secrets' and 'state'. These fields were not intended for client-side consumption and could contain credentials or other confidential data.

### Impact

An attacker with access to these API endpoints could retrieve sensitive information, including credentials or other confidential data stored within the 'secrets' or 'state' fields of Scaffolder tasks, potentially leading to further compromise.

### Key diff

```
-	        delete task.secrets;
-	        res.status(200).json(task);
+	        res.status(200).json(serializeTask(task));
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `serializeTask` function to explicitly filter and return only safe fields from a task object. The API endpoints for listing and retrieving tasks now use this serialization function, ensuring that sensitive fields like 'secrets' and 'state' are no longer exposed in API responses. Additionally, the OpenAPI schema was updated to remove these fields from the `SerializedTask` model.

## GHSA-qmw3-745m-w99g · @backstage/plugin-techdocs-node

2026-10-07 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 8.8 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from insufficient sanitization of the `markdown_extensions` field within `mkdocs.yml` configuration files. Attackers could inject arbitrary Python objects or configurations into this field, which would then be processed by the underlying MkDocs engine, leading to code execution.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve remote code execution on the server hosting the Backstage TechDocs plugin, potentially compromising the entire system or accessing sensitive data.

### Key diff

```
--- a/plugins/techdocs-node/src/stages/generate/mkdocsPatchers.ts
+++ b/plugins/techdocs-node/src/stages/generate/mkdocsPatchers.ts
@@ -390,29 +390,48 @@ export const sanitizeMkdocsYml = async (
 
     // Sanitize markdown_extensions
     const extensions = sanitized.markdown_extensions;
+    const removedEntries: string[] = [];
+    const extensionMapping = isPlainObject(extensions) ? extensions : undefined;
+    let normalizedExtensions: unknown[] | undefined;
     if (Array.isArray(extensions)) {
-      const removedEntries: string[] = [];
+      normalizedExtensions = extensions;
+    } else if (extensionMapping) {
+      normalizedExtensions = Object.entries(extensionMapping).map(
+        ([name, config]) => ({
+          [name]: config,
+        }),
+      );
+    }
+    if (extensions instanceof UnknownTag) {
+      removedEntries.push('dynamically configured extension');
+      sanitized.markdown_extensions = [];
+    } else if (normalizedExtensions) {
+      const sanitizedExtensions = normalizedExtensions.flatMap<unknown>(ext => {
+        if (ext instanceof UnknownTag) {
+          removedEntries.push('dynamically configured extension');
+          return [];
+        }
 
-      sanitized.markdown_extensions = extensions.filter(ext => {
         if (typeof ext === 'string') {
           if (isPymdownxSnippetsExtension(ext)) {
-            return true;
+            return [ext];
           }
 
           if (ext.includes(':')) {
             removedEntries.push(ext);
-            return false;
+            return [];
           }
-          return true;
+          return [ext];
         }
 
-        if (!ext || typeof ext !== 'object' || Array.isArray(ext)) {
-          return true;
+        if (!isPlainObject(ext)) {
+          removedEntries.push('malformed extension declaration');
+          return [];
         }
 
         // Check every key, not just the first, so that a multi-key mapping
         // cannot smuggle a dangerous name past the filter.
-        const extensionEntries = Object.entries(ext as Record<string, unknown>);
+        const extensionEntries = Object.entries(ext);
         const dangerousNames = extensionEntries
           .map(([extensionName]) => extensionName)
           .filter(
```

### Fix summary

The patch significantly enhances the sanitization logic for `markdown_extensions` in `mkdocs.yml`. It now explicitly filters out dangerous extension names, configurations, and dynamically configured extensions, ensuring only safe and expected values are processed. It also handles different formats of extension declarations (arrays and objects) more robustly.

## GHSA-r543-q48m-4c9j · WeasyPrint

2026-10-07 · Python · **HIGH** 8.8 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The WeasyPrint library, when processing EPS image files, implicitly relied on Pillow's integration with Ghostscript. This allowed specially crafted EPS files, which are essentially PostScript programs, to be interpreted and executed by Ghostscript, leading to arbitrary command execution.

### Impact

An attacker could embed malicious PostScript commands within an EPS image, which, when processed by WeasyPrint, would be executed by theasyPrint's Ghostscript interpreter. This could lead to remote code execution on the server hosting the WeasyPrint application.

### Key diff

```
--- a/weasyprint/images.py
+++ b/weasyprint/images.py
@@ -22,6 +22,9 @@
 # Don’t crash when converting truncated images
 ImageFile.LOAD_TRUNCATED_IMAGES = True
 
+# Don’t use Ghostscript to render possibly dangerous EPS files.
+EpsImagePlugin.gs_binary = False
+
 
 class ImageLoadingError(ValueError):
     """An error occured when loading an image.
```

### Fix summary

The patch explicitly disables Ghostscript usage for EPS image processing within Pillow by setting `EpsImagePlugin.gs_binary = False`. This prevents the execution of PostScript code embedded in EPS files, thereby mitigating the RCE vulnerability. Additionally, it adds error handling around `RasterImage` creation.

## GHSA-g8rx-f7m5-7794 · @backstage/plugin-scaffolder-backend-module-bitbucket-server

2026-10-07 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 8.5 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_WRITE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from the use of `fs.cpSync` with a `recursive: true` option, which, when combined with an insufficiently restrictive filter, allowed for directory traversal. An attacker could craft a malicious source directory containing symbolic links or specially named files that, when copied, would write to arbitrary locations outside the intended temporary directory.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve arbitrary file write capabilities on the system where the Backstage scaffolder backend is running. This could lead to remote code execution or other severe system compromises.

### Key diff

```
--- a/plugins/scaffolder-backend-module-bitbucket-cloud/src/actions/bitbucketCloudPullRequest.ts
+++ b/plugins/scaffolder-backend-module-bitbucket-cloud/src/actions/bitbucketCloudPullRequest.ts
@@ -388,8 +388,7 @@ export function createPublishBitbucketCloudPullRequestAction(options: {
         });
 
         // copy files
-        fs.cpSync(sourceDir, tempDir, {
-          recursive: true,
+        fs.copySync(sourceDir, tempDir, {
           filter: isNotGitDirectoryOrContents,
         });
```

### Fix summary

The patch replaces `fs.cpSync` with `fs.copySync` from the `fs-extra` library. The `fs.copySync` function, when used with the `filter` option, provides more robust protection against path traversal by correctly handling symbolic links and ensuring files are copied only within the intended boundaries, preventing writes to arbitrary locations.

## GHSA-xvgh-hmx8-9xxf · @backstage/plugin-scaffolder-backend

2026-10-07 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 8.5 · Pattern: `RACE_CONDITION→TOCTOU`

### Root cause

The system allowed a task to be retried without proper validation of its current state. An attacker could initiate a retry on an already 'open' task, leading to a race condition where multiple processes could attempt to update the same task, potentially corrupting its state or causing unexpected behavior.

### Impact

An attacker could exploit a race condition to manipulate the state of a task, potentially leading to denial of service for legitimate task processing or inconsistent task states within the system. This could disrupt the normal operation of the Scaffolder backend.

### Key diff

```
--- a/plugins/scaffolder-backend/src/scaffolder/tasks/DatabaseTaskStore.ts
+++ b/plugins/scaffolder-backend/src/scaffolder/tasks/DatabaseTaskStore.ts
@@ -726,10 +726,12 @@ export class DatabaseTaskStore implements TaskStore {
     const { secrets, taskId } = options;
 
     await this.db.transaction(async tx => {
-      const result = await tx<RawDbTaskRow>('tasks')
+      const updateCount = await tx<RawDbTaskRow>('tasks')
         .where('id', taskId)
-        .update(
-          {
-            ...(secrets && { secrets: JSON.stringify(secrets) }),
-            status: 'open',
-            last_heartbeat_at: this.db.fn.now(),
-          },
-          ['id', 'spec'],
-        );
-
-      for (const { id, spec } of result) {
-        const taskSpec = JSON.parse(spec as string) as TaskSpec;
+        .whereIn('status', ['cancelled', 'completed', 'failed', 'skipped'])
+        .update({
+          ...(secrets && { secrets: JSON.stringify(secrets) }),
+          status: 'open',
+          last_heartbeat_at: this.db.fn.now(),
+        });
+
+      if (Number(updateCount) !== 1) {
+        throw new ConflictError(`Task with taskId ${taskId} cannot be retried`);
+      }
+
+      const [task] = await tx<RawDbTaskRow>('tasks')
+        .where('id', taskId)
+        .select('id', 'spec');
+      const taskSpec = JSON.parse(task.spec) as TaskSpec;
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds a `whereIn('status', ['cancelled', 'completed', 'failed', 'skipped'])` clause to the update query, ensuring that a task can only be retried if it is in a terminal state. It also checks if exactly one row was updated, throwing a `ConflictError` if not, preventing retries on active tasks and mitigating race conditions.

## GHSA-826h-28h9-65hg · @backstage/plugin-auth-backend-module-oidc-provider

2026-10-07 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 8.1 · Pattern: `MISSING_VERIFICATION→SIGNATURE`

### Root cause

The OIDC authenticator in Backstage was incorrectly populating the user's email profile field directly from the OIDC userinfo response without verifying if the email address was confirmed by the identity provider. This allowed an attacker to claim any email address by manipulating the OIDC response.

### Impact

An attacker could impersonate other users by claiming their email addresses, potentially leading to unauthorized access to resources or services that rely on email for identification.

### Key diff

```
email: input.fullProfile.userinfo.email,
+      ...(input.fullProfile.userinfo.email_verified === true && {
+        email: input.fullProfile.userinfo.email,
+      }),
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the OIDC authenticator to only populate the user's email address in the profile if the `email_verified` flag in the OIDC userinfo response is explicitly `true`. This ensures that only verified email addresses are used for user identification.

## GHSA-hmp2-4m7g-22cv · @backstage/plugin-scaffolder-backend

2026-10-07 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 8.1 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTHZ→RESOURCE`

### Root cause

The original authorization logic for scaffolder actions did not correctly handle conditional permissions, specifically when a user had conditional access to a resource based on a 'path' property. The system would only check the initial input provided by the user, and if that input satisfied the conditions, it would grant access without further validation against the actual conditions. This allowed an attacker to provide a valid 'path' initially to pass the authorization check, and then the underlying action could potentially operate on a different, unauthorized path.

### Impact

An attacker could bypass authorization checks for scaffolder actions that use conditional permissions based on the 'path' property, potentially allowing them to perform actions on unauthorized resources or locations within a repository or group.

### Key diff

```
--- a/plugins/scaffolder-backend/src/scaffolder/tasks/NunjucksWorkflowRunner.ts
+++ b/plugins/scaffolder-backend/src/scaffolder/tasks/NunjucksWorkflowRunner.ts
@@ -513,12 +574,7 @@ export class NunjucksWorkflowRunner implements WorkflowRunner {
             );
           }
         }
-        if (
-          !isActionAuthorized(decision, {
-            action: action.id,
-            input: iteration.input,
-          })
-        ) {
+        if (!isActionInputAuthorized(decision, action.id, iteration.input)) {
           throw new NotAllowedError(
             `Unauthorized action: ${actionId}. The action is not allowed. Input: ${JSON.stringify(
               iteration.input,
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new function, `isActionInputAuthorized`, which performs a more thorough authorization check. For conditional permissions involving 'path' properties in 'group' actions, it extracts all possible authorized paths from the permission conditions and re-evaluates the authorization for each of these candidate paths with the user's input. This ensures that the action's input is genuinely authorized against all specified conditions.

## GHSA-rqx7-p7vv-w7hr · hydra-core

2026-10-07 · Python · **HIGH** 7.8 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The Hydra framework's instantiation mechanism allowed users to specify arbitrary Python callables via the _target_ field in configuration. While a blocklist of dangerous modules and functions existed, it was incomplete and could be bypassed by chaining allowed functions or by specifying targets that indirectly led to code execution, such as those related to process management, module loading, or deserialization.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve arbitrary code execution on the system running the Hydra application by crafting a malicious configuration file. This could lead to full system compromise, data exfiltration, or denial of service.

### Key diff

```
--- a/hydra/_internal/instantiate/_instantiate2.py
+++ b/hydra/_internal/instantiate/_instantiate2.py
@@ -2,37 +2,34 @@
 
 import copy
 import functools
-+import inspect
-+import itertools
-+import operator
 import os
-+import types
 from enum import Enum
 from textwrap import dedent
-from typing import Any, Callable, Dict, List, Sequence, Tuple, Union
-+from typing import Any, Callable, Dict, List, Sequence, Set, Tuple, Union
 
 from omegaconf import OmegaConf, SCMode
 from omegaconf._utils import is_structured_config
 
-+from hydra._internal.deprecation_warning import deprecation_warning
 from hydra._internal.utils import _locate
 from hydra.errors import InstantiationException
 from hydra.types import ConvertMode, TargetConf
 
-+ This blocklist is a best-effort, defense-in-depth stopgap. It is not a
-+ complete security boundary because application callables can indirectly
-+ dispatch operations that never appear as _target_ values.
-+
```

### Fix summary

The patch significantly expands the blocklist of dangerous targets, categorizing them into 'callback dispatch targets', 'callable wrapper targets', and 'uncontrolled execution targets'. It also introduces a new mechanism to prevent the instantiation of targets that allow arbitrary code execution, such as those related to module imports, process spawning, or deserialization, making these unallowlistable even with overrides. The patch also removes several previously blocklisted items that are now covered by the more comprehensive 'uncontrolled execution targets' list.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 111 occurrences**
> Languages: JavaScript, Python, Java, PHP, Go, Ruby, YAML, RUST, C#

## GHSA-8w7q-29mw-gf5c · @backstage/plugin-techdocs-node

2026-10-07 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.7 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The application did not properly sanitize user-controlled configuration in `mkdocs.yml` files, specifically within `markdown_extensions` and `theme` settings. This allowed attackers to inject dangerous keys like `plantuml_cmd` or other arbitrary commands, which would then be executed during the documentation generation process.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve remote code execution on the server hosting the Backstage TechDocs plugin, potentially leading to full system compromise or data exfiltration.

### Key diff

```
--- a/plugins/techdocs-node/src/stages/generate/mkdocsPatchers.ts
+++ b/plugins/techdocs-node/src/stages/generate/mkdocsPatchers.ts
@@ -291,12 +293,86 @@ export const sanitizeMkdocsYml = async (
       }
     }
 
+    // Sanitize markdown_extensions
+    const extensions = sanitized.markdown_extensions;
+    if (Array.isArray(extensions)) {
+      const removedEntries: string[] = [];
+
+      sanitized.markdown_extensions = extensions.filter(ext => {
+        if (typeof ext === 'string') {
+          if (ext.includes(':')) {
+            removedEntries.push(ext);
+            return false;
+          }
+          return true;
+        }
+
+        if (!ext || typeof ext !== 'object' || Array.isArray(ext)) {
+          return true;
+        }
+
+        // Check every key, not just the first, so that a multi-key mapping
+        // cannot smuggle a dangerous name past the filter.
+        const dangerousNames = Object.keys(
+          ext as Record<string, unknown>,
+        ).filter(key => key.includes(':'));
+        if (dangerousNames.length > 0) {
+          removedEntries.push(...dangerousNames);
+          return false;
+        }
+
+        // Strip dangerous keys from the extension's own configuration.
+        for (const extConfig of Object.values(ext as Record<string, unknown>)) {
+          if (
+            extConfig &&
+            typeof extConfig === 'object' &&
+            !ArrayOf(extConfig)
+          ) {
+            for (const dangerousKey of DANGEROUS_EXTENSION_CONFIG_KEYS) {
+              if (dangerousKey in extConfig) {
+                delete (extConfig as Record<string, unknown>)[dangerousKey];
+                removedEntries.push(dangerousKey);
+              }
+            }
+          }
+        }
+
+        return true;
+      });
+
+      if (removedEntries.length > 0) {
+        logger.warn(
+          `Removed the following dangerous entries from markdown_extensions in mkdocs.yml: ${removedEntries.join(
+            ', ',
+          )}.`,
+        );
+      }
+    }
+
     // Clear the original object and copy sanitized values back
     for (const key of Object.keys(mkdocsYml)) {
       delete (mkdocsYml as Record<string, unknown>)[key];
     }
     Object.assign(mkdocsYml, sanitized);
 
+    // Sanitize theme sub-keys
+    const theme = (mkdocsYml as Record<string, unknown>).theme;
+    if (isThemeObject(theme)) {
+      const removedThemeKeys = Object.keys(theme).filter(
+        key => !ALLOWED_THEME_KEYS.has(key),
+      );
+      if (removedThemeKeys.length > 0) {
+        for (const key of removedThemeKeys) {
+          delete (theme as Record<string, unknown>)[key];
+        }
+        logger.warn(
+          `Removed the following unsupported keys from theme configuration in mkdocs.yml: ${removedThemeKeys.join(
+            ', ',
+          )}.`,
+        );
+      }
+    }
+
     // Always rewrite to ensure clean YAML output (resolves merge keys, anchors, etc.)
     return true;
   });
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces strict allowlists for `markdown_extensions` and `theme` configuration keys in `mkdocs.yml`. It explicitly removes dangerous keys like `plantuml_cmd` and any extension configurations containing colons or other unsupported characters, preventing their execution during documentation generation.

## GHSA-f7v3-xhm6-w245 · @backstage/plugin-techdocs-node

2026-10-07 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.7 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from improper input validation in the TechDocs Markdown extension configuration. Specifically, the `sanitizeMkdocsYml` function did not adequately filter out dangerous Markdown extensions or their configurations, allowing an attacker to inject arbitrary commands via specially crafted `mkdocs.yml` files.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve remote code execution by crafting a malicious `mkdocs.yml` file that leverages dangerous Markdown extensions to execute arbitrary commands on the server hosting the TechDocs backend.

### Key diff

```
--- a/plugins/techdocs-node/src/stages/generate/mkdocsPatchers.ts
+++ b/plugins/techdocs-node/src/stages/generate/mkdocsPatchers.ts
@@ -300,6 +308,10 @@ export const sanitizeMkdocsYml = async (
 
       sanitized.markdown_extensions = extensions.filter(ext => {
         if (typeof ext === 'string') {
+          if (isPymdownxSnippetsExtension(ext)) {
+            return true;
+          }
+
           if (ext.includes(':')) {
             removedEntries.push(ext);
             return false;
@@ -313,14 +325,34 @@ export const sanitizeMkdocsYml = async (
 
         // Check every key, not just the first, so that a multi-key mapping
         // cannot smuggle a dangerous name past the filter.
-        const dangerousNames = Object.keys(
-          ext as Record<string, unknown>,
-        ).filter(key => key.includes(':'));
+        const extensionEntries = Object.entries(ext as Record<string, unknown>);
+        const dangerousNames = extensionEntries
+          .map(([extensionName]) => extensionName)
+          .filter(
+            extensionName =>
+              extensionName.includes(':') &&
+              !isPymdownxSnippetsExtension(extensionName),
+          );
         if (dangerousNames.length > 0) {
           removedEntries.push(...dangerousNames);
           return false;
         }
 
+        for (const [extensionName, extensionConfig] of extensionEntries) {
+          if (!isPymdownxSnippetsExtension(extensionName)) {
+            continue;
+          }
+
+          if (
+            extensionConfig !== null &&
+            (typeof extensionConfig !== 'object' ||
+              Object.keys(extensionConfig).length > 0)
+          ) {
+            removedEntries.push(`${extensionName} configuration`);
+          }
+          Object.assign(ext, { [extensionName]: {} });
+        }
+
         // Strip dangerous keys from the extension's own configuration.
         for (const extConfig of Object.values(ext as Record<string, unknown>)) {
           if (
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new function `isPymdownxSnippetsExtension` to specifically allow the `pymdownx.snippets` extension while strictly sanitizing its configuration to an empty object. It also enhances the filtering logic to remove any other Markdown extensions or their configurations that contain colons, which are indicative of dangerous or unsupported extensions, preventing command injection.

## GHSA-p75x-jh7p-ppcx · @backstage/plugin-techdocs-node

2026-10-07 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.7 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→XSS`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the 'extra_templates' key was allowed in the MkDocs configuration, which could be used to inject arbitrary HTML or JavaScript. This key was not properly sanitized or restricted, allowing an attacker to control the content rendered by the TechDocs backend.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) by injecting malicious HTML/JavaScript into the documentation, potentially leading to session hijacking, data theft, or defacement of the TechDocs content.

### Key diff

```
--- a/plugins/techdocs-node/src/stages/generate/helpers.ts
+++ b/plugins/techdocs-node/src/stages/generate/helpers.ts
@@ -308,7 +308,6 @@ export const ALLOWED_MKDOCS_KEYS = new Set([
   'markdown_extensions',
   'extra',
   'extra_css',
-  'extra_templates',
   // Preview controls
   'use_directory_urls',
```

### Fix summary

The patch removes 'extra_templates' from the `ALLOWED_MKDOCS_KEYS` set. This prevents users from specifying custom template directories in their MkDocs configuration, thereby eliminating the vector for injecting malicious content.

## GHSA-qgvj-qcf8-xq73 · @backstage/plugin-catalog-backend

2026-10-07 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.7 · Pattern: `SSRF→INTERNAL_ACCESS`

### Root cause

The application allowed placeholder URLs in catalog entities to be resolved using a base URL without proper validation that the resolved URL remained within the same repository. This meant that a relative URL could be crafted to point to an arbitrary external or internal resource, bypassing intended access controls.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious catalog entity to perform Server-Side Request Forgery (SSRF), potentially accessing internal network resources or external services that the Backstage backend has access to, leading to information disclosure or further attacks.

### Key diff

```
--- a/plugins/catalog-backend/src/processors/PlaceholderProcessor.ts
+++ b/plugins/catalog-backend/src/processors/PlaceholderProcessor.ts
@@ -230,7 +262,9 @@ function relativeUrl({
   } 
   try {
-    return resolveUrl(value, baseUrl);
+    const resolved = resolveUrl(value, baseUrl);
+    assertSameRepository(resolved, baseUrl);
+    return resolved;
   } catch (e) {
     // The only remaining case that isn't support is a relative file path that should be
     // resolved using a relative file location. Accessing local file paths can lead to
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new function, `assertSameRepository`, which validates that a resolved URL for a placeholder remains within the same Git repository as the base URL. It parses both URLs using `git-url-parse` and throws an error if the resource or full repository name differs, preventing cross-repository references.

## GHSA-cq7v-rfgc-5c7v · @backstage/backend-defaults

2026-10-07 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.6 · Pattern: `PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION→ROLE`

### Root cause

The system improperly preserved access restrictions when delegating service credentials. Specifically, when a service token with access restrictions was used to request a new token for a target plugin, the new token did not inherit or enforce these restrictions, allowing the delegated token to have broader access than intended.

### Impact

An attacker could leverage a service token with limited access to obtain a new token for a target plugin that bypasses the original access restrictions, potentially gaining unauthorized access to sensitive resources or performing actions they were not permitted to do.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/backend-defaults/src/entrypoints/auth/DefaultAuthService.ts
+++ b/packages/backend-defaults/src/entrypoints/auth/DefaultAuthService.ts
@@ -168,11 +169,31 @@ export class DefaultAuthService implements AuthService {
     // by checking the public keys endpoint existence.
     switch (type) {
       // TODO: Check whether the principal is ourselves
-      case 'service':
+      case 'service': {
+        const servicePrincipal =
+          internalForward.principal as BackstageServicePrincipal;
+        if (servicePrincipal.accessRestrictions) {
+          const targetRestrictions =
+            internalForward.allAccessRestrictions?.get(targetPluginId);
+          if (!targetRestrictions) {
+            throw new NotAllowedError(
+              `Access to target plugin '${targetPluginId}' is not included in token's access restrictions`,
+            );
+          }
+          if (
+            targetRestrictions.permissionNames?.length ||
+            targetRestrictions.permissionAttributes
+          ) {
+            throw new NotAllowedError(
+              `Access to target plugin '${targetPluginId}' is restricted and cannot be delegated`,
+            );
+          }
+        }
         return this.pluginTokenHandler.issueToken({
           pluginId: this.pluginId,
           targetPluginId,
         });
+      }
       case 'user': {
         const { token } =
           internalForward;
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a mechanism to explicitly check and enforce access restrictions during service credential delegation. It now stores all access restrictions and verifies that the target plugin's access is explicitly included and that no restricted permissions are being delegated, throwing a NotAllowedError if a violation is detected.

## GHSA-jg6q-3qfh-r9f8 · @insumermodel/mppx-token-gate

2026-10-07 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTH→ENDPOINT`

### Root cause

The `mppx-token-gate` library, in versions 1.0.0 through 1.0.3, incorrectly granted free access to resources based on a client-supplied `payer address` from `credential.source`. This value was treated as an asserted identity rather than requiring independent proof of control, allowing an attacker to declare any wallet address and gain unauthorized access if that wallet met the token conditions.

### Impact

An attacker could bypass the intended token-gating mechanism by simply declaring a wallet address that satisfies the token conditions, without actually owning or controlling that wallet. This could lead to unauthorized access to premium content or services.

### Key diff

```
--- a/legacy/mppx-token-gate/src/index.ts
+++ b/legacy/mppx-token-gate/src/index.ts
@@ -255,7 +255,7 @@
 
 /**
  * Wraps an mppx Method.Server. **This gate no longer grants free access.**
- *
- * Every request is delegated to the wrapped payment method.
- *
- * Versions 1.0.0 through 1.0.3 granted free access based on the payer address
- * in `credential.source` — a client-supplied value that mppx documents as "an
- * asserted identity, not independent proof of control". Nothing esta
+ * Every request is delegated to the wrapped payment method.
+ *
+ * Versions 1.0.0 through 1.0.3 granted free access based on the payer address in
+ * `credential.source` — a client-supplied value that mppx documents as "an
+ * asserted identity, not independent proof of control".
```

### Fix summary

The patch removes the free access path that was based on the client-supplied `credential.source`. The `tokenGate` wrapper now explicitly states that it "no longer grants free access" and that "every request is delegated to the wrapped payment method," implying that proper authorization and proof of control are now required.

## GHSA-jj3q-cwqj-842r · ImageSharp

2026-10-07 · C# · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `BUFFER_OVERFLOW→HEAP`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from insufficient validation of decoded run lengths during CCITT fax decompression. The `WriteBits` function, which writes pixel data to a buffer, was called with untrusted `RunLength` values without first ensuring that these lengths would not cause the write operation to exceed the allocated buffer for the current row. This allowed an attacker to provide malformed image data that would cause an unbounded write.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious TIFF image that, when processed, would lead to a heap out-of-bounds write. This could result in denial of service (application crash), information disclosure, or potentially arbitrary code execution, depending on the memory layout and the overwritten data.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/ImageSharp/Formats/Tiff/Compression/Decompressors/ModifiedHuffmanTiffCompression.cs
+++ b/src/ImageSharp/Formats/Tiff/Compression/Decompressors/ModifiedHuffmanTiffCompression.cs
@@ -53,6 +53,12 @@ protected override void Decompress(BufferedReadStream stream, int byteCount, int
 
             if (bitReader.RunLength > 0)
             {
+                // A decoded run is untrusted and must fit the current row before the unchecked bit writer is used.
+                if (bitReader.RunLength > (nuint)this.Width - pixelsWritten)
+                {
+                    TiffThrowHelper.ThrowImageFormatException("CCITT compression parsing error: decoded more pixels than the image width.");
+                }
+
                 if (bitReader.IsWhiteRun)
                 {
                     BitWriterUtils.WriteBits(buffer, bitsWritten, (int)bitReader.RunLength, this.whiteValue);
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces explicit boundary checks before calling `BitWriterUtils.WriteBits`. It now verifies that the `RunLength` of a decoded pixel run does not exceed the remaining available space in the current row buffer. If an oversized run is detected, an `ImageFormatException` is thrown, preventing the out-of-bounds write.

## GHSA-p279-5wcv-45vq · kunstmaan/bundles-cms

2026-10-07 · PHP · **HIGH** 7.2 · Pattern: `INSECURE_DEFAULT→CONFIG`

### Root cause

The Kunstmaan MediaBundle's file upload functionality relied on a blacklist of extensions to prevent the upload of executable files. However, this blacklist was incomplete and did not normalize the file extension to lowercase before checking, allowing an authenticated administrator to bypass the restriction by using a mixed-case extension (e.g., 'shell.pHp') or an unlisted executable extension.

### Impact

An authenticated administrator could upload a malicious PHP file to the server, which, when accessed, would execute arbitrary code, leading to full remote code execution on the server.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/Kunstmaan/MediaBundle/Helper/File/FileHandler.php
+++ b/src/Kunstmaan/MediaBundle/Helper/File/FileHandler.php
-        if (!empty($this->blacklistedExtensions)) {
-            $filename = preg_replace('/\.(' . implode('|', $this->blacklistedExtensions) . ')$/', '.txt', $filename);
-        }
-
-        $parts = pathinfo($filename);
-        $filename = $this->slugifier->slugify($parts['filename']);
-        if (\array_key_exists('extension', $parts)) {
-            $filename .= '.' . strtolower($parts['extension']);
+        $extension = strtolower($parts['extension']);
+
+        if (!$this->isAllowedExtension($extension)) {
+            $extension = 'txt';
         }
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces an explicit allow-list mechanism for file extensions, which, if configured, takes precedence over the blacklist. It also significantly expands the default blacklist to include many more executable extensions and ensures that all extension checks are performed on lowercased versions of the file extension to prevent bypasses.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 42 occurrences**

## GHSA-2cmg-v53w-8xfp · @backstage/plugin-scaffolder-backend-module-confluence-to-markdown

2026-10-07 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.1 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_WRITE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the application was constructing file paths using user-controlled input (parsedRepoUrl.filepath and filePathToMkdocs) without proper validation or sanitization. This allowed an attacker to inject directory traversal sequences (e.g., `../`) into the path, leading to arbitrary file system access.

### Impact

An attacker could write files to arbitrary locations on the server's file system, potentially leading to remote code execution, data corruption, or denial of service.

### Key diff

```
-      const repoFileDir = `${dirPath}/${parsedRepoUrl.filepath}`;
+      const repoFileDir = resolveSafeChildPath(dirPath, parsedRepoUrl.filepath);
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces and consistently uses the `resolveSafeChildPath` utility function. This function ensures that all file paths constructed from user input are strictly confined within a designated base directory, preventing directory traversal attacks by resolving any `../` sequences to stay within the intended boundaries.

## GHSA-4ww4-68q3-h7g5 · @payloadcms/db-mongodb

2026-10-07 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.1 · Pattern: `TYPE_CONFUSION→BYPASS`

### Root cause

The application did not properly validate the types of elements within array or blocks fields. If an attacker supplied non-object values (like null, undefined, primitives, or arrays) in these fields, the subsequent processing logic, which expected plain objects, could crash or behave unexpectedly. This could lead to a bypass of field-level write access controls.

### Impact

An attacker could potentially bypass write access controls for array or blocks fields by providing malformed data, leading to unauthorized data modification or denial of service.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/payload/src/fields/hooks/beforeValidate/promise.ts
+++ b/packages/payload/src/fields/hooks/beforeValidate/promise.ts
@@ -347,9 +348,16 @@ export const promise = async <T>({
       const rows = siblingData[field.name]
 
       if (Array.isArray(rows)) {
+        const filteredRows = stripNullRows(
+          rows,
+          'array',
+          field.name,
+          req.payload.logger,
+          siblingData,
+        )
         const promises: Promise<void>[] = []
 
-        rows.forEach((row, rowIndex) => {
+        filteredRows.forEach((row, rowIndex) => {
           promises.push(
             traverseFields({
               id,
```

### Fix summary

A new utility function, `stripNullRows`, was introduced to filter out invalid row types (non-objects) from array and blocks fields before further processing. This ensures that only plain objects are passed to subsequent validation and database operations, preventing type confusion and access bypasses.

## GHSA-5m6h-8g35-p3m7 · @quasar/app-vite

2026-10-07 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→XSS`

### Root cause

The application did not properly validate or sanitize the 'nonce' attribute used in Content Security Policy (CSP) headers for Server-Side Rendering (SSR) and Static Site Generation (SSG). An attacker could inject arbitrary HTML into the nonce attribute, leading to Cross-Site Scripting (XSS).

### Impact

An attacker could inject malicious scripts into the rendered HTML, bypassing CSP and executing arbitrary JavaScript in the user's browser, potentially leading to session hijacking, data theft, or defacement.

### Key diff

```
--- a/app-vite/templates/entry/ssr-nonce.js
+++ b/app-vite/templates/entry/ssr-nonce.js
@@ -0,0 +1,27 @@
+const nonceRE = /^[A-Za-z0-9+/_-]+={0,2}$/
+
+export function encodeHtmlAttribute(value) {
+  return String(value).replaceAll(/[&<>"]/g, char => encodeHtmlChars[char])
+}
+
+export function getNonceAttr(ssrContext) {
+  const { nonce } = ssrContext
+
+  if (nonce === void 0) return ''
+
+  if (typeof nonce !== 'string' || nonceRE.test(nonce) === false) {
+    throw new TypeError(
+      'Invalid SSR nonce. Expected a non-empty base64 or base64url value.'
+    )
+  }
+
+  return ` nonce="${encodeHtmlAttribute(nonce)}"`
+}

--- a/app-vite/lib/modes/ssr/ssr-utils.js
+++ b/app-vite/lib/modes/ssr/ssr-utils.js
@@ -10,8 +10,4 @@ export function renderStoreState(ssrContext, nonce) {
   return `<script${nonce}>window.__INITIAL_STATE__=${state};${autoRemove}</script>`
 }
 
-export function getNonceAttr(ssrContext) {
-  return ssrContext.nonce !== void 0 ? ` nonce="${ssrContext.nonce}"` : ''
-}
-
 export function logServerMessage(title, msg, additional) {
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new utility function `getNonceAttr` that strictly validates the 'nonce' value against a base64 or base64url regex and HTML-encodes it before inclusion in the HTML. It also ensures that the nonce is consistently applied to all relevant script tags, including Vue Devtools and initial state scripts.
